Q1. Digit sum \(15\) with tens digit \(8\) identifies which two-digit number?
A. \(89\)
B. \(87\)
C. \(88\)
D. \(86\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Form the digit-sum equation

\[
8+u=15\Rightarrow u=7
\]

Step 2: Write the two-digit number

Hence, the number is 87.

\[
N=10\times 8+7=87
\]

Answer Verification
Digit sum of 87 is 15.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(87\).

Shortcut

Find the ones digit from the digit sum, then place it.

\[
u=7
\]

\[
N=87
\]

Fast result: \(87\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Family: ns_missing_digit_single_rule
Situation: digit-interchange
Topology depth: 3
Exam mode: ssc

---
Q2. For the number \(480\), find the count of positive divisors?
A. \(23\)
B. \(26\)
C. \(24\)
D. \(25\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
480=2^{5}\times 3\times 5
\]

Step 2: Apply the (+1) rule on exponents

Each exponent contributes one more choice than its value.

\[
d(480)=(5+1)\times (1+1)\times (1+1)=24
\]

Answer Verification
The number of positive divisors is 24.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(24\).

Shortcut

Multiply (exponent + 1) across all prime powers.

\[
(5+1)\times (1+1)\times (1+1)
\]

Fast result: \(24\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_prime_factorization
Situation: hidden-prime-exponent
Topology depth: 4
Exam mode: pyq_plus

Q3. For two numbers \(78\) and \(60\), which common multiple is first reached?
A. \(780\)
B. \(819\)
C. \(858\)
D. \(741\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
78=2\times 3\times 13
\]

\[
60=2^{2}\times 3\times 5
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(78,60)=780
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 780.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(780\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(78,60)=\frac{78\times 60}{\operatorname{HCF}(78,60)}=780
\]

Fast result: \(780\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Family: ns_hcf_lcm_relation
Situation: constraint-number-recovery
Topology depth: 5
Exam mode: elite

Q4. Power \(11^{64}\) is tested modulo \(11\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(0\)
B. \(1\)
C. \(2\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
11\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 1.

\[
0
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(64-1)\bmod 1+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 0.

\[
11^{64}\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 0.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(0\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(64-1)\bmod 1+1=1
\]

\[
0^{64}\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Fast result: \(0\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Family: ns_remainder_after_power
Situation: range-remainder-count
Topology depth: 4
Exam mode: ssc

Q5. For exponent \(47\), what ending two digits does \(7^{47}\) have?
A. \(44\)
B. \(42\)
C. \(45\)
D. \(43\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
7\equiv 7\pmod{100}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 4.

\[
7\rightarrow 49\rightarrow 43\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(47-1)\bmod 4+1=3
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 43.

\[
7^{47}\equiv 43\pmod{100}
\]

Answer Verification
The 3rd term of the cycle is 43.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(43\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(47-1)\bmod 4+1=3
\]

\[
7^{47}\equiv 43\pmod{100}
\]

Fast result: \(43\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q8. What least number should be added to \(745\) so that the result is divisible by \(18\)?
A. \(13\)
B. \(11\)
C. \(12\)
D. \(10\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Combine the number conditions first

Optimization is done only after the divisibility or remainder condition is converted into one usable cycle.

Step 2: Move to the nearest valid boundary

The boundary step prevents picking a number that satisfies only the arithmetic but not the given range.

\[
745=18\times 41+7
\]

\[
18-7=11
\]

Answer Verification
The optimized valid value is 11.
\[
N=11
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(11\).

Shortcut

Use the combined cycle and take only the needed boundary correction.

\[
Z=11
\]

Fast result: \(11\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q9. By what least number should \(180\) be multiplied to make it a perfect square?
A. \(5\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Factorize the given number

Perfect powers are controlled by prime exponents.

\[
180=2^{2}\times 3^{2}\times 5
\]

Step 2: Complete the exponents

For a square, every exponent must be even.

\[
2^{2}\to 2^{2}
\]

\[
3^{2}\to 3^{2}
\]

\[
5^{1}\to 5^{2}
\]

Step 3: Read the required value

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
The perfect-power condition is now satisfied.
\[
N=5
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Fill only the missing prime powers; do not search through squares or cubes.

\[
M=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Family: ns_perfect_square_completion
Situation: square-divisibility-bridge
Topology depth: 5
Exam mode: elite

\[

\]

\[

\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 9.

\[
3^{68}\equiv 9\pmod{13}
\]

Final Answer

Q18. Between \(143\) and \(309\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(11\)
B. \(12\)
C. \(10\)
D. \(13\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 143 is 150.

\[
15\times 10=150
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 300 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 309.

\[
309=15\times 20+9
\]

\[
309-9=300
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{300-150}{15}+1=10+1=11
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 11.
Hence, there are 11 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(11\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{309}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{142}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
20-9=11
\]

Fast result: \(11\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Family: ns_cyclic_pattern
Situation: range-multiple-count
Topology depth: 3
Exam mode: banking

Q23. The number \(554x0\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 14.

\[
14+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+0=40
\]

\[
40\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 4.

\[
14+x=18
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
14+4=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
40\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+0\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_reverse_divisibility
Situation: lcm-divisibility-bridge
Topology depth: 4
Exam mode: elite

\[
(3+1)
\]

\[
O=4
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

\[

\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q27. The power \(8^{83}\) is very large. What are its final digit?
A. \(1\)
B. \(4\)
C. \(2\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
8\equiv 8\pmod{10}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 4.

\[
8\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 6
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(83-1)\bmod 4+1=3
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 2.

\[
8^{83}\equiv 2\pmod{10}
\]

Answer Verification
The 3rd term of the cycle is 2.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(83-1)\bmod 4+1=3
\]

\[
8^{83}\equiv 2\pmod{10}
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_cycle_length_detection
Situation: power-tower-ending
Topology depth: 5
Exam mode: elite

\[

\]

Answer Verification

Final Answer

\[

\]

Fast result: \(1155\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q34. Between \(171\) and \(307\), how many integers are divisible by \(12\)?
A. \(13\)
B. \(11\)
C. \(12\)
D. \(10\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 12 greater than 171 is 180.

\[
12\times 15=180
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 300 is the greatest multiple of 12 not exceeding 307.

\[
307=12\times 25+7
\]

\[
307-7=300
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 12.

\[
\frac{300-180}{12}+1=10+1=11
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 11.
Hence, there are 11 multiples of 12 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(11\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{307}{12}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{170}{12}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
25-14=11
\]

Fast result: \(11\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Situation: range-multiple-count
Topology depth: 3
Exam mode: ssc

\]

\]

Hence, remainder = 6.

\[

\]

Final Answer

\]

\[

Q56. Between \(173\) and \(347\), how many integers are divisible by \(8\)?
A. \(24\)
B. \(22\)
C. \(23\)
D. \(21\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 8 greater than 173 is 176.

\[
8\times 22=176
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 344 is the greatest multiple of 8 not exceeding 347.

\[
347=8\times 43+3
\]

\[
347-3=344
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 8.

\[
\frac{344-176}{8}+1=21+1=22
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 22.
Hence, there are 22 multiples of 8 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(22\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{347}{8}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{172}{8}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
43-21=22
\]

Fast result: \(22\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q59. When \(13^{52}\) is divided by \(9\), what remainder is left?
A. \(3\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
13\equiv 4\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 3.

\[
4\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(52-1)\bmod 3+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 4.

\[
13^{52}\equiv 4\pmod{9}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 4.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(52-1)\bmod 3+1=1
\]

\[
4^{52}\equiv 4\pmod{9}
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q80. Power \(2^{34}\) is tested modulo \(13\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(10\)
B. \(11\)
C. \(9\)
D. \(12\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
2\equiv 2\pmod{13}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 12.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 12\rightarrow 11\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(34-1)\bmod 12+1=10
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 10.

\[
2^{34}\equiv 10\pmod{13}
\]

Answer Verification
The 10th term of the cycle is 10.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(10\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(34-1)\bmod 12+1=10
\]

\[
2^{34}\equiv 10\pmod{13}
\]

Fast result: \(10\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q88. Between \(110\) and \(258\), how many integers are divisible by \(8\)?
A. \(20\)
B. \(18\)
C. \(21\)
D. \(19\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 8 greater than 110 is 112.

\[
8\times 14=112
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 256 is the greatest multiple of 8 not exceeding 258.

\[
258=8\times 32+2
\]

\[
258-2=256
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 8.

\[
\frac{256-112}{8}+1=18+1=19
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 19.
Hence, there are 19 multiples of 8 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(19\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{258}{8}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{109}{8}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
32-13=19
\]

Fast result: \(19\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Situation: range-multiple-count
Topology depth: 3

9\times 13=117
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 279 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 283.

\[
283=9\times 31+4
\]

\[
283-4=279
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{279-117}{9}+1=18+1=19
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 19.
Hence, there are 19 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(19\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{283}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{108}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
31-12=19
\]

Fast result: \(19\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Topology depth: 3

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(88,136)=1496
\]

\]

Q112. Power \(13^{50}\) is tested modulo \(9\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(7\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(9\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
13\equiv 4\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 3.

\[
4\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(50-1)\bmod 3+1=2
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 7.

\[
13^{50}\equiv 7\pmod{9}
\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 7.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(50-1)\bmod 3+1=2
\]

\[
4^{50}\equiv 7\pmod{9}
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Situation: power-remainder-cycle
Topology depth: 3
Exam mode: banking

A. \(2\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(3\)
D. \(4\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
7\equiv 2\pmod{5}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 4.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(83-1)\bmod 4+1=3
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 3.

\[
7^{83}\equiv 3\pmod{5}
\]

Answer Verification
The 3rd term of the cycle is 3.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(83-1)\bmod 4+1=3
\]

\[
2^{83}\equiv 3\pmod{5}
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q150. Between \(187\) and \(361\), how many integers are divisible by \(6\)?
A. \(29\)
B. \(30\)
C. \(28\)
D. \(31\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 187 is 192.

\[
6\times 32=192
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 360 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 361.

\[
361=6\times 60+1
\]

\[
361-1=360
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{360-192}{6}+1=28+1=29
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 29.
Hence, there are 29 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(29\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{361}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{186}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
60-31=29
\]

Fast result: \(29\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q155. Between \(113\) and \(289\), how many integers are divisible by \(6\)?
A. \(30\)
B. \(31\)
C. \(29\)
D. \(32\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 113 is 114.

\[
6\times 19=114
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 288 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 289.

\[
289=6\times 48+1
\]

\[
289-1=288
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{288-114}{6}+1=29+1=30
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 30.
Hence, there are 30 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(30\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{289}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{112}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
48-18=30
\]

Fast result: \(30\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Family: ns_cyclic_pattern
Situation: range-multiple-count
Topology depth: 3
Exam mode: banking

\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q157. By what least number should \(360\) be multiplied to make it a perfect cube?
A. \(77\)
B. \(75\)

D. \(74\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Factorize the given number

Perfect powers are controlled by prime exponents.

\[
360=2^{3}\times 3^{2}\times 5
\]

Step 2: Complete the exponents

For a cube, every exponent must be a multiple of 3.

\[
2^{3}\to 2^{3}
\]

\[
3^{2}\to 3^{3}
\]

\[
5^{1}\to 5^{3}
\]

Step 3: Read the required value

\[
x=75
\]

Answer Verification
The perfect-power condition is now satisfied.
\[
N=75
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(75\).

Shortcut

Fill only the missing prime powers; do not search through squares or cubes.

\[
M=75
\]

Fast result: \(75\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q160. For two numbers \(24\) and \(78\), which common multiple is first reached?
A. \(312\)
B. \(328\)
C. \(343\)
D. \(296\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
24=2^{3}\times 3
\]

\[
78=2\times 3\times 13
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(24,78)=312
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 312.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(312\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(24,78)=\frac{24\times 78}{\operatorname{HCF}(24,78)}=312
\]

Fast result: \(312\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q161. For the number \(30\), find the product of its positive divisors?
A. \(1012500\)
B. \(1215000\)
C. \(810000\)
D. \(891000\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
30=2\times 3\times 5
\]

Step 2: Count the divisors

\[
d(30)=(1+1)\times (1+1)\times (1+1)=8
\]

Step 3: Use the product-of-divisors identity

\[
\prod d\mid 30 d=30^{d(30)/2}=30^{8/2}=810000
\]

Answer Verification
The product of all positive divisors is 810000.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(810000\).

Shortcut

After counting divisors, raise n to half the divisor count.

\[
30^{8/2}=810000
\]

Fast result: \(810000\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q163. Power \(7^{90}\) is tested modulo \(9\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(1\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(0\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
7\equiv 7\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 3.

\[
7\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(90-1)\bmod 3+1=3
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[
7^{90}\equiv 1\pmod{9}
\]

Answer Verification
The 3rd term of the cycle is 1.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(90-1)\bmod 3+1=3
\]

\[
7^{90}\equiv 1\pmod{9}
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Situation: range-remainder-count
Topology depth: 4
Exam mode: ssc

\]

\[

\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{60}{27}\right\rfloor
\]

Step 2: Add the quotient terms

\[
20+6+2=28
\]

Answer Verification
Highest power of 3 dividing 60! is 28.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(28\).

Shortcut

Legendre sum of floor quotients only.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{60}{3}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{60}{9}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{60}{27}\right\rfloor
\]

Fast result: \(28\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\]

Hence, remainder = 4.

\]

\]

\[
2^{106}\equiv 4\pmod{17}
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\[

Q180. Between \(207\) and \(360\), how many integers are divisible by \(18\)?
A. \(11\)
B. \(9\)
C. \(10\)
D. \(8\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 18 greater than 207 is 216.

\[
18\times 12=216
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 360 is the greatest multiple of 18 not exceeding 360.

\[
360=18\times 20+0
\]

\[
360-0=360
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 18.

\[
\frac{360-216}{18}+1=8+1=9
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 9.
Hence, there are 9 multiples of 18 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{360}{18}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{206}{18}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
20-11=9
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q182. Power \(2^{56}\) is tested modulo \(13\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(9\)
B. \(10\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(11\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
2\equiv 2\pmod{13}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 12.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 12\rightarrow 11\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(56-1)\bmod 12+1=8
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 9.

\[
2^{56}\equiv 9\pmod{13}
\]

Answer Verification
The 8th term of the cycle is 9.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(56-1)\bmod 12+1=8
\]

\[
2^{56}\equiv 9\pmod{13}
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q190. Between \(152\) and \(317\), how many integers are divisible by \(6\)?
A. \(26\)
B. \(29\)
C. \(27\)
D. \(28\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 152 is 156.

\[
6\times 26=156
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 312 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 317.

\[
317=6\times 52+5
\]

\[
317-5=312
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{312-156}{6}+1=26+1=27
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 27.
Hence, there are 27 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(27\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{317}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{151}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
52-25=27
\]

Fast result: \(27\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Situation: range-multiple-count
Topology depth: 3
Exam mode: banking

Q191. For the number \(20\), find the product of its positive divisors?
A. \(9600\)
B. \(7200\)
C. \(8000\)
D. \(8800\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
20=2^{2}\times 5
\]

Step 2: Count the divisors

\[
d(20)=(2+1)\times (1+1)=6
\]

Step 3: Use the product-of-divisors identity

\[
\prod d\mid 20 d=20^{d(20)/2}=20^{6/2}=8000
\]

Answer Verification
The product of all positive divisors is 8000.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(8000\).

Shortcut

After counting divisors, raise n to half the divisor count.

\[
20^{6/2}=8000
\]

Fast result: \(8000\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q213. When \(11^{44}\) is divided by \(9\), what remainder is left?
A. \(3\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
11\equiv 2\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 6.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(44-1)\bmod 6+1=2
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 4.

\[
11^{44}\equiv 4\pmod{9}
\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 4.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(44-1)\bmod 6+1=2
\]

\[
2^{44}\equiv 4\pmod{9}
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q220. Between \(215\) and \(303\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(5\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 215 is 225.

\[
15\times 15=225
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 300 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 303.

\[
303=15\times 20+3
\]

\[
303-3=300
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{300-225}{15}+1=5+1=6
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 6.
Hence, there are 6 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{303}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{214}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
20-14=6
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Situation: range-multiple-count
Topology depth: 3

Q240. Between \(156\) and \(332\), how many integers are divisible by \(6\)?
A. \(29\)
B. \(32\)
C. \(30\)
D. \(31\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 156 is 156.

\[
6\times 26=156
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 330 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 332.

\[
332=6\times 55+2
\]

\[
332-2=330
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{330-156}{6}+1=29+1=30
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 30.
Hence, there are 30 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(30\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{332}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{155}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
55-25=30
\]

Fast result: \(30\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q260. Between \(146\) and \(248\), how many integers are divisible by \(6\)?
A. \(17\)
B. \(18\)
C. \(16\)
D. \(19\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 146 is 150.

\[
6\times 25=150
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 246 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 248.

\[
248=6\times 41+2
\]

\[
248-2=246
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{246-150}{6}+1=16+1=17
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 17.
Hence, there are 17 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(17\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{248}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{145}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
41-24=17
\]

Fast result: \(17\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\[

\]

\[

\]

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(40,80)=80
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 80.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(80\).

Shortcut

\[

\]

Fast result: \(80\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q262. Power \(7^{28}\) is tested modulo \(13\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(9\)
B. \(10\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(11\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
7\equiv 7\pmod{13}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 12.

\[
7\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 11\rightarrow 12\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(28-1)\bmod 12+1=4
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 9.

\[
7^{28}\equiv 9\pmod{13}
\]

Answer Verification
The 4th term of the cycle is 9.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(28-1)\bmod 12+1=4
\]

\[
7^{28}\equiv 9\pmod{13}
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Situation: counter-cycle
Topology depth: 3

\]

Step 2: Write the two-digit number

Hence, the number is 37.

\[
N=10\times 3+7=37
\]

Answer Verification
Digit sum of 37 is 10.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(37\).

Shortcut

Find the ones digit from the digit sum, then place it.

\[
u=7
\]

\[
N=37
\]

Fast result: \(37\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

A. \(2074\)
B. \(1555\)
C. \(1728\)
D. \(1901\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
12=2^{2}\times 3
\]

Step 2: Count the divisors

\[
d(12)=(2+1)\times (1+1)=6
\]

Step 3: Use the product-of-divisors identity

\[
\prod d\mid 12 d=12^{d(12)/2}=12^{6/2}=1728
\]

Answer Verification
The product of all positive divisors is 1728.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(1728\).

Shortcut

After counting divisors, raise n to half the divisor count.

\[
12^{6/2}=1728
\]

Fast result: \(1728\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q290. With tens \(7\) and ones \(1\), a two-digit number is written. What difference appears after reversing?
A. \(54\)
B. \(55\)
C. \(53\)
D. \(56\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 7+1=71
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 1+7=17
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |7-1|=9\times 6=54
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 54.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(54\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |7-1|=54
\]

Fast result: \(54\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q291. What is the highest power of \(2\) that divides \(75!\)?
A. \(72\)
B. \(70\)
C. \(73\)
D. \(71\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Count powers of 2 in n!

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{75}{2}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{75}{4}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{75}{8}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{75}{16}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{75}{32}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{75}{64}\right\rfloor
\]

Step 2: Add the quotient terms

\[
37+18+9+4+2+1=71
\]

Answer Verification
Highest power of 2 dividing 75! is 71.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(71\).

Shortcut

Legendre sum of floor quotients only.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{75}{2}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{75}{4}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{75}{8}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{75}{16}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{75}{32}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{75}{64}\right\rfloor
\]

Fast result: \(71\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 324 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 329.

\[
329=9\times 36+5
\]

\[
329-5=324
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{324-198}{9}+1=14+1=15
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 15.
Hence, there are 15 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(15\).

\]

\[

\]

Fast result: \(15\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\]

Q307. Power \(13^{38}\) is tested modulo \(11\). Which remainder is obtained?

B. \(4\)
C. \(2\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
13\equiv 2\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 10.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(38-1)\bmod 10+1=8
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 3.

\[
13^{38}\equiv 3\pmod{11}
\]

Answer Verification
The 8th term of the cycle is 3.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

\[
p=(38-1)\bmod 10+1=8
\]

\[
2^{38}\equiv 3\pmod{11}
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q310. Between \(128\) and \(213\), how many integers are divisible by \(12\)?
A. \(8\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(9\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 12 greater than 128 is 132.

\[
12\times 11=132
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 204 is the greatest multiple of 12 not exceeding 213.

\[
213=12\times 17+9
\]

\[
213-9=204
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 12.

\[
\frac{204-132}{12}+1=6+1=7
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 7.
Hence, there are 7 multiples of 12 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{213}{12}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{127}{12}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
17-10=7
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Situation: remainder-constrained-digit
Topology depth: 4
Exam mode: ssc

\[

\]

\[

\]

\[

\]

\]

\]

\]

\[

\]

Final Answer

\[

\]

Fast result: \(27\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q341. Between \(205\) and \(356\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(10\)
B. \(11\)
C. \(9\)
D. \(12\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 205 is 210.

\[
15\times 14=210
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 345 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 356.

\[
356=15\times 23+11
\]

\[
356-11=345
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{345-210}{15}+1=9+1=10
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 10.
Hence, there are 10 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(10\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{356}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{204}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
23-13=10
\]

Fast result: \(10\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q362. When \(7^{32}\) is divided by \(11\), what remainder is left?
A. \(4\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
7\equiv 7\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 10.

\[
7\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(32-1)\bmod 10+1=2
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 5.

\[
7^{32}\equiv 5\pmod{11}
\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 5.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(32-1)\bmod 10+1=2
\]

\[
7^{32}\equiv 5\pmod{11}
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q374. Between \(138\) and \(250\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(6\)
B. \(9\)
C. \(7\)
D. \(8\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 138 is 150.

\[
15\times 10=150
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 240 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 250.

\[
250=15\times 16+10
\]

\[
250-10=240
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{240-150}{15}+1=6+1=7
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 7.
Hence, there are 7 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{250}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{137}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
16-9=7
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\]

\]

\[

\]

\]
\[

\[

\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[

\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

\[

\]

\[

\]

\[

\]

Q392. Between \(201\) and \(308\), how many integers are divisible by \(6\)?
A. \(18\)
B. \(19\)
C. \(17\)
D. \(20\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 201 is 204.

\[
6\times 34=204
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 306 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 308.

\[
308=6\times 51+2
\]

\[
308-2=306
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{306-204}{6}+1=17+1=18
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 18.
Hence, there are 18 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(18\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{308}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{200}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
51-33=18
\]

Fast result: \(18\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\[

\]

\]

Fast result: \(504\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

\]

A. \(35\)
B. \(38\)

D. \(37\)
Answer: C

\[
N_1=10\times 5+1=51
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 1+5=15
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |5-1|=9\times 4=36
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 36.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(36\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |5-1|=36
\]

Fast result: \(36\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q431. Between \(156\) and \(239\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 156 is 165.

\[
15\times 11=165
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 225 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 239.

\[
239=15\times 15+14
\]

\[
239-14=225
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{225-165}{15}+1=4+1=5
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 5.
Hence, there are 5 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{239}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{155}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
15-10=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_divisibility_multi_condition
Situation: lcm-divisibility-bridge
Topology depth: 4
Exam mode: elite

Q433. Power \(2^{59}\) is tested modulo \(9\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(5\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
2\equiv 2\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 6.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(59-1)\bmod 6+1=5
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 5.

\[
2^{59}\equiv 5\pmod{9}
\]

Answer Verification
The 5th term of the cycle is 5.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(59-1)\bmod 6+1=5
\]

\[
2^{59}\equiv 5\pmod{9}
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q441. Between \(145\) and \(251\), how many integers are divisible by \(9\)?
A. \(11\)
B. \(12\)
C. \(10\)
D. \(13\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 145 is 153.

\[
9\times 17=153
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 243 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 251.

\[
251=9\times 27+8
\]

\[
251-8=243
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{243-153}{9}+1=10+1=11
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 11.
Hence, there are 11 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(11\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{251}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{144}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
27-16=11
\]

Fast result: \(11\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Situation: missing-digit-rule
Topology depth: 2
Exam mode: banking

Q442. Two cyclic events repeat every \(132\) and \(72\) minutes. After how many minutes will they occur together again?
A. \(832\)
B. \(871\)
C. \(752\)
D. \(792\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
132=2^{2}\times 3\times 11
\]

\[
72=2^{3}\times 3^{2}
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(132,72)=792
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 792.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(792\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(132,72)=\frac{132\times 72}{\operatorname{HCF}(132,72)}=792
\]

Fast result: \(792\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

A. \(9\)
B. \(10\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(11\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 3+4=34
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 4+3=43
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |3-4|=9\times 1=9
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 9.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |3-4|=9
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\]

Multiply the geometric-sum factor for each prime power.

\[
\sigma(108)=280
\]

Answer Verification
The sum of all positive divisors is 280.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(280\).

Shortcut

Write prime powers once, then multiply the σ-factors.

\[

\]

Fast result: \(280\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q464. When \(7^{60}\) is divided by \(9\), what remainder is left?
A. \(0\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(1\)
D. \(2\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
7\equiv 7\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 3.

\[
7\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(60-1)\bmod 3+1=3
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[
7^{60}\equiv 1\pmod{9}
\]

Answer Verification
The 3rd term of the cycle is 1.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(60-1)\bmod 3+1=3
\]

\[
7^{60}\equiv 1\pmod{9}
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q475. Between \(187\) and \(331\), how many integers are divisible by \(6\)?
A. \(23\)
B. \(26\)
C. \(24\)
D. \(25\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 187 is 192.

\[
6\times 32=192
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 330 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 331.

\[
331=6\times 55+1
\]

\[
331-1=330
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{330-192}{6}+1=23+1=24
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 24.
Hence, there are 24 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(24\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{331}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{186}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
55-31=24
\]

Fast result: \(24\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

2^{56}\equiv 4\pmod{9}
\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 4.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(56-1)\bmod 6+1=2
\]

\[
2^{56}\equiv 4\pmod{9}
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q488. Two cyclic events repeat every \(40\) and \(104\) minutes. After how many minutes will they occur together again?
A. \(546\)
B. \(572\)
C. \(494\)
D. \(520\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
40=2^{3}\times 5
\]

\[
104=2^{3}\times 13
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(40,104)=520
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 520.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(520\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(40,104)=\frac{40\times 104}{\operatorname{HCF}(40,104)}=520
\]

Fast result: \(520\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Family: ns_three_number_hcf_lcm
Situation: three-number-alignment
Topology depth: 4
Exam mode: ssc
